countdown**, `Dungeon.mobsToChampion` (reset to 8 whenever it reaches 0, decremented on every
eligible spawn, assigning a champion exactly when it hits 0 with the challenge active) - not the
"flat 10% roll" this row's own text above still called the accepted remaining approximation.
Fixed both at once: `rollForChampion` (new, `actors/monsterSpawn.ts`) reproduces the exact
countdown rule with no exclusion of any kind, `mobsToChampion` is now real per-run scene state
(`DungeonScene.mobsToChampion`, persisted in `SaveShape`, restored on load - 0 on a fresh run,
matching Java's own zero-valued static field, which this port's rule already treats as
"reset to 8 on first use"), and the depth/kind exclusion branch is deleted outright rather than
kept as a still-claimed-real simplification. **Lesson, same shape as the Test Subject/Tested
Hypothesis one already recorded above: re-verify a "found and fixed, matches Java" claim against
the actual current source before extending or trusting it forward - this one had been sitting as
verified for two days.** Verified headlessly (`tools/scratch/champion-counter-check.mjs`, 4
assertions): the challenge being off never assigns a champion but still advances the counter
(matching Java's own RNG-order comment); the challenge being on assigns exactly the 8th, 16th and
24th eligible spawn in a 24-call run, not a probability; a save-restored mid-sequence counter (3)
correctly needs only two more spawns rather than resetting to eight; the assigned type is always
one of the real six. `tsc`/`build`/all suites green.
`tools/scratch/champion-roll-livecheck.mjs` (browser-only, unavailable this session) still
asserts the old ~10%-with-exclusions shape and is annotated with what a future browser pass
should assert instead (`champions === 37` of 300, no exclusion checks) rather than silently left
to mislead the next reader.
**Correction, 2026-09-15: the "fourth correction" immediately above (deleting the by-depth
exclusion outright, and flattening the interval to a plain reset-to-8) was itself wrong, and the
code has already moved past it without this row being updated to say so.** `src/actors/
monsterSpawn.ts`'s current `rollForChampion` (re-checked directly, not from memory of the
paragraph above) restores both halves that paragraph removed: `championExcluded` still blocks
Crab/Thief/Guard/Bat below depths 3/4/7/9 (`GreatCrab`/`Bandit` inheriting via `baseKind`,
matching Java's `instanceof` subclassing), and a successful assignment adds back `8 - min(20,
scalingDepth()-1)/10` rather than a flat `8` - the real interval shrinking from 8 to 6 as depth
rises from 1 to 201+, per `ChampionEnemy.java`'s exact formula. The function's own header comment
already narrates why: a still-earlier pass, the same day as the "fourth correction", had misread
the mechanic from this checkout's plain working-tree `ChampionEnemy.java` (which sits near
`v2.1.4`) instead of `git show refs/tags/v3.3.8:...`, and both the exclusion-removal and the
flat-8 interval were regressions from that misread, caught and reverted before being reported as
done - `tools/scratch/champion-counter-check.mjs` was written against an interim two-argument
`rollForChampion(counter, active)` shape from partway through that back-and-forth and is stale
against the current four-argument signature (`mobsToChampion, challengeActive, excluded,
depth`); it is not part of `npm run verify` and was not updated this pass, but should not be read
as describing current behaviour. **Lesson, worth stating plainly since this is the second time
this exact row has self-corrected a self-correction: when this checkout's local
shattered-pixel-dungeon working tree and a tagged ref disagree, the tagged ref is authoritative
for this port's target version - a bare path read without `git show refs/tags/<tag>:<path>` can
silently return the wrong game version's behaviour.** No new browser verification was run this
pass; the claim above is sourced from re-reading the current TypeScript directly, which is
authoritative for "what the code does now" independent of any run history.
**"No visual treatment exists for any champion type" is now closed too, same day.** `ChampionEnemy
.fx()`'s real `target.sprite.aura(color)` is a persistent glow-ring primitive this port's
`TintedSprite` has no equivalent for (only a flat `tint`/`setColorAdd`); `spawnMonster` now
applies a flat `sprite.tint` in the buff's own real colour (`CHAMPION_TINT`, all six values from
`ChampionEnemy.java`'s own declaration order) as the practical stand-in, the same shape this file
already reaches for elsewhere (an armed noisemaker's `sprite.tint = 0xff4444`). `tsc`/`build`/all
suites green; browser verification (a spawned champion actually showing its real colour, not just
the tint assignment compiling) owed per ROADMAP.md section 10. |
| `GnollExile`/`HermitCrab` alt-table swap mobs (`Gnoll.java`/`Crab.java`, tag `v3.3.8`) | none - flagged by an audit pass over `MobSpawner`/`RegularLevel`, not yet implemented | **Not ported.** Java's alt-mob table (`RegularLevel.RARE_MOB_ALT_TABLE`-style swap) can replace an ordinary `gnoll`/`crab` roster spawn with `GnollExile` (a ranged-throwing gnoll variant) or `HermitCrab` (a shell-swap crab variant) on the deeper floors of their normal range; neither kind exists in this port's `MonsterId` set, `monsters.mwl`, or any spawn table, so the swap itself is also unmodeled (moot until the kinds exist). No messages, sprites, or stats have been ported for either. **2026-09-21:** the rest of the population plan audited exact against `MobSpawner`/`RegularLevel` (`addRareMobs` depths 4/9/14/19 at 0.025, `mobLimit` 3+depth%5+Int(3) with LARGE ceil(1.33x), floor-1 eight per `createMobs`, `int(0,3)` exclusive-max matching `Int(3)`) with first committed `planMonsterPopulation` pins in `test:simulation`; all 11 `RARE_ALTS` entries now accounted (8 in-table, chaos via `Elemental.random`, these 2 unported), and the missing `RatSkull` multiplier note added at the alt-chance site (no trinket system, always default 1). |
| `Dungeon.LimitedDrops` decay on top of `MOB_LOOT`'s flat chance | `src/monsters.ts`'s `LIMITED_DROP_DECAY`, `SewersScene.limitedDrops` (persisted with the run, not the floor - matches `Dungeon.LimitedDrops` being a run-lifetime static, reset only on a new game), the `kill()` loot-roll loop | Fetched `Bat.java`/`Necromancer.java`/`Guard.java`/`Slime.java`/`Skeleton.java`/`Thief.java`/`Swarm.java`/`DM200.java`/`Golem.java`/`Shaman.java`/`Warlock.java` to confirm every kind's real `lootChance()` override. **Bat/Necromancer/Guard are now ported**: each already had the right base chance in `MOB_LOOT` (`1/6` potion, `0.2` potion, `0.2` armor respectively), just missing Java's own further per-drop decay - `(7-n)/7`, `(6-n)/6`, `(1/3)^n` - where `n` is how many times *that exact drop* has already happened this run. Browser-verified live: killing 400 fresh bats produced exactly 7 drops before the counter hit 7 and the chance floor to `(7-7)/7 = 0` (matching Java's own hard cutoff, since that formula is linear and reaches exactly zero); the same test on necromancers produced exactly 6 drops, matching `(6-n)/6`'s own zero point; guards (whose `(1/3)^n` never mathematically reaches zero, only asymptotes) accumulated a real, incrementing counter without a hard cutoff, as expected. **`dm200`/`golem`/`shaman` are now ported too, found and fixed in the same follow-up pass**: `dm200`'s and `golem`'s `MOB_LOOT` entries had the wrong base chance outright (`{chance:0.125, kind:'armor'}` for both, an unconfirmed-guess bug with no derivation from Java's real `lootChance = 0.2` field) - both corrected to `0.2`, with their own `(1/3)^n` decay now wired (same shape as Guard's). `shaman`'s base chance (`0.03`) was already correct, just missing its own `(1/3)^n` decay, now added. Browser-verified live: 2000-3000 kills of fresh dm200/golem/shaman reached decay counters of 6/7/4 respectively, consistent with the `(1/3)^n` asymptotic falloff (no hard zero-point, unlike Bat/Necromancer's linear formulas). **Still not modeled**: Java's `Random.oneOf(WEAPON, ARMOR)` 50/50 category pick for dm200/golem - both always produce the `'armor'`-kind ground item here, a pre-existing simplification not newly introduced. **`slime`/`skeleton`/`thief`/`swarm` are now ported too, closing the last base-drop gap this row tracked**: each now has a real `MOB_LOOT` entry plus its own decay - `slime` (`0.2` base, weapon simplified to the shared weapon-as-`'armor'` stand-in dm200/golem already use, `(1/4)^n` via `SLIME_WEP`), `skeleton` (`1/6` base, same weapon-as-`'armor'` stand-in - Java's is any-tier `WEAPON` rather than Slime's `WEP_T2`, not distinguished here since this port has no per-tier weapon ground-item kind at all, `(1/3)^n` via `SKELE_WEP`), `thief` (`0.03` base, Java's `Random.oneOf(RING,ARTIFACT)` collapsed to the single `'ring'` kind rather than folding into the shared `'wand'` kind Artifact normally maps to - the `'wand'` choice would make it indistinguishable from a real wand pickup, `(1/3)^n` via `THEIF_MISC`), and `swarm` (`1/6` base `'potion'`, `(5-n)/5` via `SWARM_HP` - Java's own `1/(6*(generation+1))` term is not reproduced since this port's monster AI has no Swarm-split mechanic, so `generation` is always Java's own `0` case here). **`Warlock`'s `WARLOCK_HP` slot is now ported too, in its own dedicated `kill()` branch rather than the generic `MOB_LOOT` table** - it isn't a `lootChance()` override (Warlock's own top-level `0.5` chance has no `LimitedDrops` decay in Java at all), it's a separate roll on *which* potion class drops. This needed its own branch because this port's generic `'potion'` bag id always resolves to a full heal on quaff (`quaffPotion`'s own simplification), so routing Warlock through the shared `MOB_LOOT` `'potion'` kind the way Bat/Necromancer/Scorpio do would make every Warlock kill a guaranteed free heal - the exact opposite of real Java, where a Warlock drop is a genuine `PotionOfHealing` only on `Random.Int(3)==0 && Random.Int(8) > WARLOCK_HP.count` (reusing the shared `limitedDrops` map under a `warlock` key for that counter) and a redrawn non-`PotionOfHealing` class otherwise - reproduced here as a uniform pick among the 7 other potion ids this port already models by id (`potionStrength`/`potionFlame`/`potionMindVision`/`potionInvis`/`potionPurity`/`potionExperience`/`potionLevitation`). **`Scorpio` is now ported too, the same fix applied to a simpler case**: its real `createLoot()` is a flat `0.5` chance (no `LimitedDrops` counter at all) that redraws until the potion class is neither Healing nor Strength - reproduced as a uniform pick among the remaining 6 modeled potion ids, in its own `kill()` branch next to Warlock's. **`Succubus` is now ported too, the scroll-side equivalent**: its real `createLoot()` redraws until the scroll class is neither Identify nor Upgrade - this port's generic `'scroll'` bag id resolves to exactly those two, the same mismatch. Fixed the same way, in its own `kill()` branch, picking uniformly among the other 10 non-excluded ids. **Found and fixed a separate, genuinely pre-existing crash surfaced while wiring this**: `APPEARANCE_TABLES.scroll`'s `kinds` list never included `scrollTransmutation` at all, even though `sourceInventoryItem`'s own generic `ScrollOf* -> 'scroll'+Name` rename already produces exactly that id for any floor-generated `ScrollOfTransmutation` (Library rewards, plain floor loot) - so an unidentified one reaching `itemDisplayName` would throw inside `mwg`'s `Appearances.appearanceOf` (`"scrollTransmutation" is not in appearance category "scroll"`), a real, live crash risk with no prior report, not something this loot work introduced. Fixed by adding it as the table's 13th kind, with `labels` duplicating its first rune name onto this port's own synthetic pre-resolution `'scroll'` placeholder (a kind with no real Java counterpart at all) rather than inventing a fake 13th SPD rune name. **`snake`/`gnoll`/`crab` are now ported too, closing three more totally-missing base-drop gaps found in a full sweep of every real spawnable kind's `loot` field**: `Snake.loot = Generator.Category.SEED` (`0.25`, this port's real `'seed'` ground-item kind), `Gnoll.loot = Gold.class` (`0.5`), `Crab.loot = MysteryMeat.class` (`0.167`, `1/6`) - none had any `MOB_LOOT` entry at all before. `Rat` and `Goo` were also checked and confirmed to have no `loot` field in Java at all, so correctly still have no entry. **Correction 2026-09-22 (ACP #306):** `monk`'s base chance was 0.1 with no Java behind it - `Monk.lootChance` is 0.083, now the row's value. **Found, not fixed, correctly scoped as bigger**: real Java's `Elemental` is abstract with 4 concrete subtypes picked via `Elemental.random()` for a normal spawn (`FireElemental`: `PotionOfLiquidFlame`, `1/8`; `FrostElemental`: `PotionOfFrost`, `1/8`; `ShockElemental`: `ScrollOfRecharging`, `1/4`; `ChaosElemental`: `ScrollOfTransmutation`, guaranteed `1`) - this port's single generic `'elemental'` id (no subtype AI/sprite distinction at all, a pre-existing, larger simplification) has no `MOB_LOOT` entry for any of them. Not attempted this pass: `PotionOfFrost` has no dedicated ground-item id or appearance-table entry of its own yet (it already falls through to Purity's effect on quaff, a documented gap elsewhere), so a naive drop would either need a fake virtual subtype roll at kill time (a real design choice, not a quick fix) or risk repeating the exact `appearanceOf`-throws-on-an-unmapped-kind bug `scrollTransmutation` just had, if implemented carelessly. **2026-09-14:** removed `monsters.ts`'s dead `LEGACY_LIMITED_DROP_DECAY` object literal (the same ten formulas, hand-duplicated, with no reader left anywhere once `limitedDropDecay`'s MWL migration landed); its Java-citation comments now live on `MWL_LIMITED_DROP_DECAY`/`LIMITED_DROP_DECAY` instead. |

| `Challenges.java` selection plus `AscensionChallenge.statModifier` per-mob table | `src/challenges.ts`, title Settings > Challenges, `ASCENSION_MOD` | Challenge ids/names/descriptions are selectable and persisted between runs. **Audited every challenge for whether its toggle actually does anything, having just found `champion_enemies` didn't**: `stronger_bosses` is real and **boss-only** - 18 call sites covering boss HP (Goo 100->120, Tengu 200->250, DM300 300->400, King 300->450, Pylon 50->80), DM300's cooldown and gas multiplier, King's phase-2 thresholds, Goo's heal increment, Tengu's ability deck, and `CavesBossLevel`'s trap chance plus final-pylon count. **This row used to claim the challenge "activates the Java multiplier table (already live)" - that was a bug written down as a feature.** The two are unrelated Java mechanisms, and the wiring pointed the *AscensionChallenge* per-mob table at this challenge, so selecting Stronger Bosses multiplied every ordinary mob's accuracy **and** damage by up to x10 (a rat). Found and fixed 2026-09-12; see the `AscensionChallenge` row below. `champion_enemies` now gates the champion roll (fixed this pass, see the `ChampionEnemy` row); `darkness` is now ported too - real `Level.viewDistance` drops from `8` to `2`, and since this port already uses one shared `VIEW_RADIUS` constant for both the hero's own FOV and every monster's `seesHero`/AI sight radius (the same shape real Java's own light-casting array backs both alike with), a single new `viewRadius()` helper reading `isChallengeEnabled('darkness')` covers every call site at once. `no_armor` is now ported too: real `Armor.DRMin()`/`DRMax()` drop to a flat `0` and `1+tier+lvl(+augment, not modeled here)` respectively under the challenge, instead of the normal `lvl`/`tier*(2+lvl)` scaling - `syncHeroFromStats`'s existing `hero.armor = [min, max]` assignment now branches on `isChallengeEnabled('no_armor')`; the Barkskin-talent addend stays unconditional on both branches, since it's this port's own additive layer on top of either formula, not part of Java's `DRMin`/`DRMax` bodies. `no_healing` is now ported too: fetched `PotionOfHealing.java` to confirm real `heal()`'s challenge branch grants no `Healing` buff at all - instead `pharmacophobiaProc()` sets a fresh `Poison` at the hero's own `4 + lvl/2` level. This port's `poison` buff has no separate stack-level dimension (a pre-existing simplification - every source just sets the shared flat duration, dealing a fixed 1-2 dmg/turn regardless), so `4 + floor(lvl/2)` is applied as that duration instead, the closest faithful substitute within the existing model. `cure()`'s own poison/burning/weakness/vulnerable/cripple clear still runs first either way, matching Java's `apply()` calling both unconditionally; the `restored_willpower`/`restored_agility`/`restored_nature` talent triggers correctly do not fire under the challenge, since real Java gates them on the `Healing` buff itself existing. New `port.log.pharmacophobia` EN/FR keys (no real Java message string was available in this checkout to reuse, unlike most other new lines here). `no_herbalism` is now ported too: real `Plant.Seed.onThrow()` falls through to a plain thrown-item drop under the challenge instead of ever planting - this port has no throw-to-cell targeting at all, so `plantSeed()` simply refuses the action without consuming the seed, the closest faithful equivalent. **Found and fixed three unrelated hardcoded-English strings in the same function while adding this gate** - `plantSeed()`'s three existing log lines (`This cell cannot grow a plant.` / `The seed has no known plant effect.` / the plant-success line) had never gone through `t()` at all, a real, pre-existing i18n gap this project's own audit convention exists to catch; all three plus the new `no_herbalism` line are now real `port.log.*` keys with EN+FR entries, reconfirmed by rerunning the full `port.*` literal-key audit (0 missing in either locale, 224 keys now in use). `swarm_intelligence` is now ported too: fetched `Mob.java`'s `Sleeping`/`Wandering` AI states to confirm the real hook - the instant any enemy mob notices the hero, it beckons every other non-paralyzed, not-yet-`HUNTING` enemy mob within 8 tiles of *itself* toward the hero too, rather than each mob only ever noticing independently. Wired into the existing wake check (right where `monster.sleeping` flips `false`); this port has no `HUNTING`/`WANDERING` state machine, so "not yet `HUNTING`" is approximated as "not already awake-and-`seesHero`," and the beckon itself reuses the exact `sleeping=false`/`seesHero=true` stand-in `ScrollOfRage`'s own beckon already established. **`no_food` is now ported**: real `Food.satisfy()` subtracts `HUNGRY` energy (300 for ordinary food, 150 for MysteryMeat), dividing that energy by three under `NO_FOOD`; `eatFood()` reproduces this in the MWG hunger model. **Correction 2026-09-19:** the "flat meat-heal simplification" this sentence used to preserve is removed - no Java food heals, so `MysteryMeat.effect()`'s debuff roll runs instead. **`no_scrolls` is now ported**: Java's `Dungeon.souNeeded()`/`Level.create()` allocation is reproduced as three guaranteed Upgrade Scrolls per five-floor chapter, with every second allocation suppressed while its run counter still advances. The port's generic floor allocator had no pre-existing guaranteed-scroll queue, so this is integrated as an equivalent post-generated-item placement with the same chapter budget and random floor selection. |
| `AscensionChallenge.statModifier` per-class table (`Rat` 10 down to `Scorpio` 1.1) | `ASCENSION_MOD` in `src/simulation/combat.ts`, `ascensionOn()` gate | **Simplified (audited 2026-09-21):** Java applies this table only while the hero holds the `AscensionChallenge` buff, i.e. during the post-victory ascent (`AscensionChallenge.java`: `if (Dungeon.hero == null || Dungeon.hero.buff(AscensionChallenge.class) == null) return 1;`), and this port does not model the ascent, so nothing sets the flag and the multiplier is always 1 in-game. It used to be gated on the *Stronger Bosses* challenge instead, which made every ordinary mob up to x10 stronger - see the challenges row above. The table itself was also unreliable: `skeleton`/`thief` were 6 instead of 5, `dm100` 5 instead of 4.5, and fourteen mobs Java names (`bat`, `brute`, `shaman`, `spinner`, `dm200`, `ghoul`, `elemental`, `warlock`, `monk`, `golem`, `ripperDemon`, `succubus`, `eye`, `scorpio`) were missing. Now Java's 25 classes flattened onto this port's 40 ids, since Java resolves by `isAssignableFrom` (a subclass inherits its parent) while this port models subclasses partly as MWL `BASE_KIND_ALIASES` variants and partly as first-class `MonsterId`s; a `verifyCombat` check pins the flattened table and asserts each alias-table variant matches its base kind's value. The table is flattened from Java's `isAssignableFrom` inheritance onto this port's ids and pinned by `verifyCombat`; accuracy, attacker damage, and defender `drRoll()` now use Java's multiplier and rounding order. The `Ratmogrify.TransmogRat` and `AscensionBuffBlocker` exemptions remain unmodeled because their source markers are absent.

| Four concrete Elemental kits (`FireElemental`, `FrostElemental`, `ShockElemental`, `ChaosElemental`) | `Creature.elementalType`, `randomElementalType`, `elementalRangedTurn`, `mobOnHit`, `kill()` elemental loot branch, potion appearance table | Simplified but ported at the kit boundary: subtype selection now matches Java (Chaos 1/50, otherwise Fire/Frost/ Shock at 40/40/20% - **corrected 2026-09-19, 38th matrix**: the chaos roll was `Random.int(0, 50) === 0`, and mwg's `int` is inclusive at both ends, so chaos came up 1/51 with the wrong draw shape; now `Random.float() < 1 / 50` like Java's own `Float()` draw, the `RatSkull` multiplier at its default 1, pinned live plus structurally in `test:simulation`), melee/ranged fire and frost effects are active, and Fire/Frost/Shock/Chaos produce their Java loot outcomes (1/8, 1/8, 1/4, guaranteed). Shock Blindness uses Daze and Chaos's cursed-wand delegation uses an existing harmful status because those subsystems are absent; subtype-specific sprites and chained lightning geometry remain. **Corrected 2026-09-12: the ranged bolt used to *also* deal `NormalIntRange(20, 25)` damage, which Java's never does.** `Elemental.zap()` is `hit(this, enemy, true)` -> `rangedProc(enemy)` and nothing else - `FireElemental` reignites Burning (skipped in water, at the ranged explicit 4 since 2026-09-22 rather than the table-default 8 - ACP #332), `FrostElemental` calls `Freezing.freeze`, `ShockElemental` applies `Blindness.DURATION/2f` - so an Elemental's threat at range is the status alone, and the phantom 20-25 (which also made this port's elementals far deadlier than Java's) is gone. Browser-verified live: 25 zaps of each type left the hero's HP untouched while applying the status every time. **Ported 2026-09-21:** `ShockElemental.meleeProc`'s arc is live - `planShockElementalArc` reproduces Java's `Shocking.arc` radius/order and the `round(damage*0.4)` factor, and the scene lands each hit armor-piercing (`ch.damage` never rolls armor), keeping a dry-land defender out of the set and water defenders in (pinned in `test:simulation`). Not modelled still: the Chaos cursed-wand table. **Ported 2026-09-22 (ACP #818 F1-F3):** opposite-element attaches backlash instead of landing (`elementalBacklashApplies`/`applyElementalBacklash`, damage plus refusal at every `addBuff`/`reigniteBuff` call and the five chill direct-write paths, presented/killed through the scene hook - see the Core-combat row); `Char.Property` damage halves are live through two shared gates (`icyDamageHalved` for frost bolts on frost elementals, `electricDamageHalved` for lightning/shocking/electricity/shock-sourced hits on shock elementals, DM100, the Pylon and BrightFist - wand, blob and both arc seams, all `Math.round`), which also closes the DM100 instance of #796 and the Pylon note of #814; fire melee now `reigniteBuff`s (prolong, never truncating an already-long burn) instead of setting the table 8. Stated remainder: the ICY buff-immunity half (Frost/Chill never attaching to frost elementals) is still open, as are FIERY damage halves and ShockingDart/Potential seams. |
| `INFINITE_ACCURACY`/`INFINITE_EVASION` short-circuits (surprise attacks, untargetable defenders) | `INFINITE_ACCURACY`/`INFINITE_EVASION`, sleeping-target surprise, NPC unkillability | Ported |

| `Char.java`'s `paralysed`/`rooted`/`flying`/`invisible` fields (gate movement in `Hero.actMove`/`Char.move`, trap/chasm/water interaction, and `Char.canInteract`'s stealth check) | `Creature.buffs`, `onAction`/`moveTo`/`triggerTrapAt`/`fallThroughChasm`, `rollHit`, `statusPane.ts` | Ported in the model used by this game: paralysis consumes the creature's turn; roots block movement but not attacks; levitation bypasses the port's pressure/ground traps and (as of the chasm-falling row below) chasms too; and invisibility is timed, displayed, makes distant monsters lose target, gives surprise, and dispels on attack. Buff durations also persist through save/load. There is still no water-hazard terrain in this port's model, so Levitation has no such interaction to apply there - the earlier note that chasm cells didn't exist either was stale; `isChasmCell`/`fallThroughChasm` already model chasm terrain and predate this correction. |
| `Door.leave()` via `Char.move()` (doors shut behind whoever walks through) | `leaveDoor` (`src/scenes/dungeon/environmentFireTraps.ts`), the five voluntary step paths, `tools/verifyDoors.mjs` | Ported (2026-09-20): leaving an open door shuts it unless a heap lies on it or another creature is still on the cell (`chars <= 1` with the mover counted, matching Java calling before `pos = step`); teleports and knockbacks set position directly and never close doors. The hero, monster, and ally voluntary steps pass their pre-move cell; tile restitch follows `bumpDoor`, and the next input `refresh()` re-observes. This restores breaking line of sight through doorways for surprise attacks. |
| `Door.enter()` via `Level.occupyCell()` (a shut door swings open under whoever steps onto it) | `moveTo` (`src/scenes/dungeon/bosses/bossLogic.ts`) | Ported (2026-09-20): any creature ending a move on a shut, unlocked, non-secret door opens it (sound only when the cell is in the hero's view), flying or not, like Java. Before this only the hero opened doors (through `bumpDoor`); a monster following the hero walked through the door he had just closed behind him, leaving it drawn shut with the monster inside. `leaveDoor` above shuts it again once the last occupant leaves. **Divergence (deliberate):** bodiless creatures (`IMMATERIAL_KINDS` in `src/monsters.ts`: ghost, wraith, dust wraith, spectral necromancer) pass through a wooden door without opening it, where Java opens it for every character, flying or not; bees and flies are physical and open doors like any other mob. |
| `Key.depth`/`isSimilar()` and heap/door presentation (tag `v3.3.8`): every key only counts toward the depth it was found on; unopened heaps use `CHEST`/`LOCKED_CHEST`/`CRYSTAL_CHEST` sprites and locked doors use the locked/crystal terrain frames | `pickupPayload` (`src/items/groundPickup.ts`), `pickupCrystalKey`/`spawnGroundItem` (`src/scenes/dungeon/npcShopBlacksmith.ts`), `bumpDoor` and the locked-chest checks, HUD key count (`deathSaveRefresh.ts`), `CHEST_FRAME`/`LOCKED_CHEST_FRAME`/`CRYSTAL_CHEST_FRAME` (`src/dungeonConstants.ts`) | **Ported/fixed 2026-09-21:** keys are depth-stamped, instance-separated, consumed from the matched entry, and counted across all current-depth entries (pinned in `test:items`). The visual audit against `ItemSpriteSheet.java` found and fixed two real bugs, both confirmed against a fresh extraction of the real `v3.3.8` sheet before touching anything (every cell involved was already byte-identical in this port's own `items.png` - only the frame-lookup wiring was wrong, no art needed sourcing). First: the port was drawing the contained reward's frame on unopened chests, instead of Java's container frames 36/37/38; `spawnGroundItem` now selects those frames until the heap unlocks. Second: `ironKey`'s own icon was wrong twice over - its bag-slot frame fell back to the generic "?" placeholder (unlisted in `itemSpecificFrames`), and its ground-pile frame was wired to 56, the golden key's own frame (`ItemSpriteSheet.IRON_KEY/GOLDEN_KEY/CRYSTAL_KEY = MISC_CONSUMABLE+7/8/9` = 55/56/57); both the `itemFrames` and `itemSpecificFrames` MWL tables now carry all three keys at their real frames, live-verified showing three visually distinct icons. Java's stale off-depth key displays as a black icon; this port intentionally omits stale icons rather than silently making them usable. The SkeletonKey/WornKey halves and their sealed-exit terrain remain not ported. |
| `ItemSpriteSheet.GOLD`/`UNCOLLECTIBLE` row ground-icon frames (tag `v3.3.8`) | `itemFrames` MWL table (`src/content/item-rules.mwl`), `spawnGroundItem` | **Fixed 2026-09-21, found auditing the same row the key fix lives in.** A ground gold pile was wired to frame 18 (`UNCOLLECTIBLE+0` by the tag `v3.3.8` formula) - live-verified to render as a fully blank/invisible sprite in this port's own `items.png`. The real coin-pile art sits two cells earlier, at 16, confirmed both by eye (an unmistakable pile of gold coins) and by re-rendering a spawned pile in-browser after the fix; wired to the pixel actually present rather than the formula, since this port's sheet does not lay out that row identically to the tag `v3.3.8` source in every cell (`ENERGY`/ blank slots shift by the same two cells; dewdrop is audited in the separate row below). **Fixed 2026-09-21:** `petal` was likewise wired to frame 39, which rendered the wrong/blank cell; a cell-by-cell comparison against the v3.3.8 sheet located the exact petal pixels at this port's cell 20, and the mapping now uses 20. |
| `ItemSpriteSheet.DEWDROP`/`SANDBAG` ground-icon frames (tag `v3.3.8`) | `itemFrames` MWL table (`src/content/item-rules.mwl`), `spawnGroundItem` | **Fixed 2026-09-21:** the copied sheet's layout drift put the exact Java dewdrop pixels at cell 19 (not raw frame 21) and the exact sandbag pixels at cell 21 (not raw frame 23); the mappings now use those verified cells. |
| `Mob.Hunting.act()`'s pursuit of the last known position, across doors; `Mob.defenseProc()`'s `aggro` + `target = enemy.pos` | `doorAwareLevel` (`src/scenes/dungeon/monsters/doorAwareLevel.ts`) wrapped around the floor pathfinder in `enterLevel`; the hero-hit block in `attack()` (`combatResolution.ts`) | Ported (2026-09-20), fixing a player report that mobs did not follow the hero once he was out of view. **Cause:** a shut door is impassable terrain in this port (the hero bumps it open), so the pathfinder found no route across one; a mob chasing the hero lost its path the moment he went through a door (which shuts behind him) and gave up on the spot. Java's `DOOR` is passable and mobs open it by walking in. Pathing now treats a shut, unlocked, non-secret door as passable (path length across a door went 0 -> 2), and `moveTo` opens it under the mob; spawning, drops, teleports and the hero's bump-to-open still read the plain terrain. Also: a mob struck by the hero now takes his position as its hunt target even when it cannot see him (Java sets `target = enemy.pos` in `defenseProc`), so a thrown dart from out of view draws it in. The rest of the pursuit (walk to the last seen cell, then wander) was already Java's. |
| `Level.drop()` onto a chasm -> `Dungeon.dropToChasm()` / `droppedItems` and `GameScene`'s arrival landing | `spawnGroundItem` chasm check, `dropToChasm` / `landFallenItems` (`src/scenes/dungeon/coreSpawnTiles.ts`), `src/scenes/dungeon/fallenItems.ts`, `saveRun`/`loadRun` | Ported (2026-09-20): any item dropped on a chasm cell (loot from a mob killed over a chasm, a thrown item that lands in one) is queued for the depth below and, on arrival there (new floor or revisit), lands on a random free cell out of the hero's view, else beside the hero. Before this the item simply rested on the chasm cell. Queued items are saved with the run. **Simplified:** Java shatters a fallen potion, plants a fallen seed and shatters a honeypot on landing; here they land as ordinary items. Not applied in the mining branch or from depth 26. |
| `Chasm.java`'s `heroLand()` (fall-damage consequences, once a floor/chasm cell exists to fall through) | `fallThroughChasm`/`landFromChasm` | Ported: `Cripple`, the real `Bleeding` intensity formula, and the real `max(HP/2, NormalIntRange(HP/2, HT/4))` upfront damage, run through the same hero-damage absorption pipeline (Tenacity/Barrier/Iron Will/Deathless Fury) every other source uses; a chasm death correctly triggers `kill()`. **Ported 2026-09-21 (chasm half of the source-class distinction):** `Bleeding.act()`'s three-way `source` branch (`Chasm.class`/`Sacrificial.class`/`Sickle.HarvestBleedTracker.class`) needed a `bleedSource` field on `Creature` (`combat.ts`), since this port's merged buff-damage tick previously had no way to tell which producer's bleed actually killed the hero and defaulted every non-burning DoT death to the generic `'poison'` cause - so a hero who survived the fall itself but died a turn or two later from its lingering `Bleeding` was misreported as a poison death, missing `DEATH_FROM_FALLING`. `setBleeding` now takes an optional source (only written alongside a winning, higher level, matching Java's `set(level, source)`), tagged at the chasm fall (`'chasm'`), the Sacrificial curse proc (`'sacrificial'`), and the Sickle Harvest talent's bleed (`'harvestBleed'`); the death-tick dispatch in `turnLoopAiming.ts` now maps a fatal `bleedSource === 'chasm'` bleed to the `'falling'` cause, closing the delayed-death gap. **Still not ported:** the other two branches' actual consequences - `Sacrificial.class` should book `Badges.validateDeathFromFriendlyMagic()`, a badge this port has no catalogue row for at all (would need a new `badges.mwl` row plus i18n, out of scope here), and `Sickle.HarvestBleedTracker.class` should call `MeleeWeapon.onAbilityKill()`, a stat/credit hook this port has no equivalent system for anywhere. Both sources are now tagged (so the data is there once those systems exist) but neither consequence fires yet. Blood-splash presentation remains unported. **Ported 2026-09-21:** the fall plays Java's `FALLING` cue (bundled but never cued), the landing shakes (already wired - the row's "no camera-shake system" note was stale), and a lethal landing books `DEATH_FROM_FALLING` (new `death_falling` catalogue row at Java's own cell 21, mapped in `kill()`). **Correction, 2026-09-19: `FeatherFall`'s fall-negation (`consumeFeatherFall`) is not missing** - it was already ported and is called from this same `landFromChasm` site (see that class's own row); this row's older claim predates that and was never updated. **Corrected 2026-09-21** - the row's old closing line is stale: `Chasm.heroJump()`'s confirmation dialog IS ported (`confirmChasmJump`, `simulation/chasmJump.ts`): Java's own strings, the 200ms anti-misclick guard, the still-on-the-same-cell latch check, "yes" latching plus same-turn re-entry and "no"/path-cancel matching `interrupt()`, with the latch clearing first in the fall. Levitation skips the warning like Java's `flying` bypass, except Java still warns when the levitation is about to detach mid-step, which this port cannot time and treats as flying. |

| `Bleeding` buff and `Sacrificial.proc()` / chasm source applications | `simulation/buffs.ts`, `setBleeding`, `landFromChasm`, `attack` | Ported for the active Chasm and Sacrificial sources: intensity keeps the strongest application, ticks with Java's `NormalFloat(level/2, level)` then `round`, and healing cures it. Remaining Bleeding sources (Sickle harvest, monster/trap effects) and source-specific death badges/blood visuals are not yet ported. |
| `Char.canEnterCell()` monster treatment of `Terrain.CHASM` | `populate`, `spawnPortedMobs`, `standableCellIn` | Ported: monsters and NPCs are now rejected from raw chasm cells even though the coarse MWG terrain map exposes those cells as passable for the hero's falling interaction. This closes a live generation bug where a ported-floor monster could render over unsupported black/chasm terrain. |
| `Char.move` / `Actor.findChar` occupancy invariant (two live actors never share a cell) | `moveTo` (`src/scenes/dungeon/bosses/bossLogic.ts`) plus per-path selection gates, `findSharedCell` (`src/simulation/actorCollision.ts`), `tools/verifyActorCollision.mjs` | Ported (2026-09-21): audited every `moveTo`/`stepMonster` path against Java, where `Char.move()` itself writes unconditionally and the gates live at selection (`Mob.getCloser`/`canPassTo` refuse `findChar(cell) != null`, `Hero.getCloser` steps only onto empty cells and turns occupants into Attack/Interact). This port mirrors that placement: hero steps via `runMovement` (occupied targets become interact/attack/door), monster/ally steps via `blocked` sets holding all other creatures (`decideMonsterAI`/`pathfinder.find`, `wanderBlocked`, `fleeStep`, `nearestFreeCell`), forced shoves stop at the first occupied cell, and teleports land on `randomFreeCell`/free-filtered cells with refusal when none is free. Scripted transitions mirror Java's direct `pos` writes (Caves/Halls seals with Java's own fallbacks, Tengu arena/death) - the Caves 32-try cap hardens Java's spin-until-passable loop against hangs, and the Halls filter adds an `inside` check Java lacks. The standing check is `findSharedCell` plus five selection-layer pins (blocked sets, flee/blink/summon skips) in `test:simulation`. |
| `Hero.getCloser()` bump precedence (rooted vs closed door) | `planMovement` (`src/simulation/movement.ts`) | **Fixed 2026-09-21** (audit finding): Java's `getCloser()` checks `rooted` first, before any bump logic, so a rooted hero bumping a closed door gets no interaction at all (the door stays shut). `planMovement` checked the door before the root, so a rooted hero bumping a door here opened it. Reordered to check `isRooted()` before `closedDoorAt()`; the occupant/attack branch is unaffected either way, since Java routes attacks through the occupant check before `getCloser` runs. |
| `Dungeon.flee`'s chasm exclusion (`Hunting.getFurther`, tag `v3.3.8`) | `fleeStep` (`src/simulation/wandering.ts`), shared by `stepAway` (GnollTrickster/Thief/Spinner/Scorpio/acidic retreats) and `fleeCrystalMimic` | **Fixed 2026-09-21** (muse's audit #58): `Dungeon.flee`'s own `findPassable` map excludes chasm cells from a retreat's candidate set, matching `nearestFreeCell`/`isPatrolTargetValid`'s existing `isChasm` gates elsewhere in this same module - `fleeStep` alone had no such gate, so a retreating monster could pick a chasm cell as its "farthest open neighbour," which `moveTo` then silently refused, wasting the turn instead of actually retreating. `isChasm` moved from `SummonCellContext`'s own fields onto the shared base `FleeStepContext` (the scene's `fleeStepContext()` builder already had `isChasmCell` on hand for the summon-search context, so no new binding was needed), pinned in `verifyActorCollision.mjs` and `verifyItemWorkflows.mjs`. |
| Combat rolls/damage/status/turn-timing verification (roadmap section 9) | `tools/verifyCombatRolls.mjs` (`test:simulation`), `TURN_COSTS.search` (`src/simulation/heroActions.ts`, read by the live loop through `dispatchHeroAction`), cost pins (`tools/verifyHeroActions.mjs`) | Ported (2026-09-21): deterministic formula-shape pins (two-roll hit sweep, inclusive multiplier boundaries, damage endpoints + scratch floor, multiplier chain order, Preparation best-of, status deal/redraw/decrement/expire, helper rounding). Real bug fixed in the pass: searching spent 1 turn against Java's `TIME_TO_SEARCH = 2f` (`spendAndNext`), halving monster turns during searches - now 2. The shared hero-turn seam now passes the actual cost: Hunger advances once per actor tick (a two-turn search runs 296 to 298), equipped and spare wands recharge once per tick with the missing-charge curve recomputed per tick, and the legacy tome resource receives the full tick cost. The complete hero-owned buff/effect pass (`applyBuffDamage`) now loops once per actor tick, including DoTs, healing-over-time, artifact/cape/book/talisman/rose/corpse-dust recharge, deferred damage, shields and scheduled hazards. Fire evolution now also runs once per consumed actor tick, and the post-pipeline `healingEvasion`, `enhancedRings`, `seerShotCooldown`, and `seerCells` countdowns subtract the full cost. The scheduler now charges ordinary hero actions by their actual cost, while TimeBubble/Hourglass absorb the same full cost; Hourglass's `turnsToCost` loop also consumes charge units at the Java `2f` boundary. Live Playwright instrumentation confirmed a two-turn scheduler spend records cost `2`, a two-turn frozen action consumes two bubble units without scheduling automatic actors, and Hourglass moves `timeBubbleTurns 4 -> 2`/`turnsToCost 2 -> 0` with zero page errors. Assassin KO executes (2026-09-21 audit): the preparationCanKo gate (strict HP fraction vs the rank KO threshold, boss/divisor-5 included) fires in combatResolution, dealing lethal damage with the talent message - Ported. **Corrected same day, a real bug in this row's own "no port model" claim**: `Char.hit()` (tag `v3.3.8`, lines 523-537 and 540-554) runs the *identical* `enemy.HP = 0` + `BruteRage.detach()` block for both the Assassin KO and Combined Lethality execute paths - this port had only wired the Brute/ArmoredBrute revival suppression (`clExecuted`, now renamed `heroExecuted`) for Combined Lethality, so an Assassin one-shotting a raging Brute still triggered its revive-with-shield instead of the kill sticking. Now both paths suppress it, pinned by a source check in `verifyArmorAbilities.mjs`. **Still not ported**, and correctly so: `enemy.damage(-1, this)`/`DeathMark.processFearTheReaper(enemy)` only run in Java's `!enemy.isAlive()`-after-`HP=0`-still-true else-branch - an edge this port's `kill()` has no equivalent of, since every creature here dies outright once `hp <= 0` - so both are dead code in practice, not a live gap. **2026-09-21:** eating now costs Java's `Food.TIME_TO_EAT = 3f` (`TURN_COSTS.eat`, split out of the 1-cost ranged bucket - quaff `TIME_TO_DRINK = 1f` and read `TIME_TO_READ = 1f` verified correct there), or 1 with any of Java's six meal talents (`MEAL_TALENTS`, `eatingTime() = TIME_TO_EAT - 2`), pinned in `test:simulation` (`verifyHeroActions`); the talent flag now threads through live dispatch (codex-01 follow-up: `HeroActionPorts.hasMealTalent`, scene resolves `MEAL_TALENTS` ranks, runtime command carries it), so talented eating costs 1 with no residual. TimeBubble count question closed with no bug: `reset()` sets 7 *including* the triggering action's own spend (`left = turns + 1`), and the port's trample-triggered `setTimeBubble(7)` flows through `finishHeroTurn` → `spendScheduledTurn(cost)` the same way, leaving 6 free actions on both sides. |
| `undefined`-as-unimplemented-code audit (roadmap section 9) | `noImplicitReturns` (`tsconfig.json`), `tools/audit-undefined.mjs` (wired into `npm run check`) | Ported (2026-09-21): all hits outside `src/generated/` classify as nullish/compare idioms, `T \| undefined` lookup-or-missing returns, optional context hooks, or documented Java-mirroring no-ops; the only TODO/FIXME lines cite Java's own FIXME guards (implemented beside each citation); no empty stub bodies or swallowed catches. The compiler flag proves no value-returning path falls off the end (the pre-enable probe was already clean); the script fails on bare TODO/FIXME markers and empty-message throws. |
| MWL under-usage audit (roadmap section 9): duration constants vs `buffDurations` | `BUFF_DURATION` reads at the six call sites, `tools/verifyClericSpells.mjs` `single-sourced` check, simulation-suite `lethalHasteCooldown` row | Ported (2026-09-21): `DIVINE_SENSE_TURNS`/`HOLY_BUFF_TURNS`/`GUIDING_PRIEST_COOLDOWN_TURNS`/`LANCE_COOLDOWN_TURNS`/`AURA_DURATION`/`LETHAL_HASTE_COOLDOWN` duplicated MWL rows with Java-verified equal values (`DivineSenseTracker.DURATION` 50, `HolyWepBuff`/`HolyArmBuff` `DURATION` 50, priest-cooldown 50f, lance 30f, `AuraBuff.DURATION` 20f, lethal-haste 100f) and now read `BUFF_DURATION` - behavior-neutral by construction. Deliberately kept: per-source literals that differ from the table mirror Java's own applied-vs-class-`DURATION` splits (ShieldOfLight 4-vs-5, vulnerable 5-vs-20, Radiance paralysis 3 vs `Paralysis.DURATION` 10). |

| The Cleric spell system entire (30-spell `ClericSpell` roster, base cost 1, tome menu/quickspell, `chargeCap = min(level+3, 10)`, recharge scaling, `LIGHT_READING`, `MagicImmune` gate - **tiers 1-3 plus Ascended spell-cast shield/counters, Judgement and Flash ported 2026-09-21, see below**), the three armor abilities (`Trinity` base 25 + per-effect costs, `PowerOfMany` ally empowerment, `AscendedForm` base 50 + 30-shield/10-turn `AscendBuff`), and the PRIEST/PALADIN subclasses | `ascendedBarrier`/`ascendedTurns`/`ascendedSpellCasts`/`ascendedFlashCasts` save and turn seams; `activateAscendedForm`; `spendTomeForCast`; `resolveJudgement`; `resolveFlash`; `trinityBodyDuration`/`trinityMindItemLevel`/`trinitySpiritRingLevel`/`trinitySpiritArtifactLevel`/`trinityChargeUsePerEffect` | **Simplified/Not ported (2026-09-21):** the base `AscendedForm` choice is offered and usable, with Java's 30-point separate shield, 10-turn lifetime, 50-charge base cost, invisibility dispel and one-turn action. While active, every tome cast now applies Java's `10*chargeUse` shield and increments the Ascended cast history. `Judgement` is available only while AscendedForm is active, damages every visible hostile for its rank/cast-history-scaled normal roll, spends 3 tome charges and a turn, then resets the cast history. `Flash` is available only while active, teleports to a valid empty passable cell within `2+rank`, spends `2+flashCasts` charges and a turn, and increments its rising cost counter. **Divergence (deliberate):** Java's Priest branch also applies GuidingLight's `Illuminated` effect; this port has no shared illumination add-on in this path yet. Java also gates Flash against `mapped`/`visited` map memory, which this port does not persist; passability and occupancy are enforced instead. **Trinity arithmetic is now ported and pinned:** BodyForm's 13/20/27/33/40-turn duration, MindForm's +2..+6 item level, SpiritForm's 0..4 ring and 2..10 artifact levels, and Trinity's 1x/1.4x/2x per-effect charge multipliers are pure helpers only. **BodyForm effect dispatch ported 2026-09-22 (ACP audit-01 #469):** while `trinityForm === 'body'` and `trinityTurns > 0`, the hero's melee `heroOnHit` now falls back to the stored `trinityBodyAffix` in `combatResolution.ts`'s existing affix-resolution chain (`unstableDelegated ?? weaponAffix ?? trinityBodyProc`) - the stored affix rides the same proc path a weapon affix would, with its per-effect charge already spent at commit time, so it never overrides a real weapon/Unstable/thrown-share affix, only fills a bare-fisted or affix-less swing. MindForm and SpiritForm still commit a cosmetic 1-turn window with no effect (they need item-level plumbing for armor/ring/artifact virtual levels that does not exist yet) - remain not ported. `DivineIntervention`, Trinity's PowerOfMany, the port's `T`-key heal (5+2/lvl through slow charges), and the remaining PRIEST/PALADIN behavior stay explicitly unported. **Closed 2026-09-22, Judgement's own cast flash:** `GameScene.flash(0x80FFFFFF)` (`Judgement.onCast()`) is a screen-wide light-blend flash fired once per cast, ahead of the per-target damage loop - `dungeonScene.ts`'s new `screenFlash`/`screenFlashOverlay` (a screen-space overlay on `stage` directly, not `camera.world`, so it covers the viewport regardless of camera position) now fires from `resolveJudgement` with a stated-approximate `0.3s` fade (Java's own `Fader` duration lives in a `noosa` framework class outside this checkout's history, so the exact value could not be read from source). Live-verified: a scripted cast (talent/subclass/AscendedForm gates bypassed for the test) sets the flash state and deals real Judgement damage; the overlay renders visibly via a forced-render pixel check. |
 | Tier-1 Cleric spells (`GuidingLight`/`HolyWeapon`/`HolyWard`) and tier-1 talents (`SatiatedSpells`/`HolyIntuition`/`SearingLight`/`ShieldOfLight`) plus the `HolyTome` charge/spend/upgrade economy (`HolyTome.java`, `ClericSpell.java`, `Talent.java`, the five spell/talent classes, tag `v3.3.8`) | `src/simulation/clericSpells.ts` (cap `min(level+3, 10)`, costs 1/2/2, `spendTomeCharge` exp/upgrade, talent formulas `1+2*points`/`4-points`/tracker turns), `src/items/holyTome.ts` (purse/row/aim/bag-picker gates), `useHolyTome`/`holyTomeContext`/`resolveGuidingLight`/`castHolyBuff`/`resolveHolyIntuition`/`resolveShieldOfLight`/`consumeSatiatedSpells`/`spendTomeForCast`, the MWL Cleric T1 row, the recharge-table entry + cap + grant + save/load, the `attack()`/`defenseProc()`/`onFoodEaten` hooks, and the `shieldOfLightTarget` save field | **Ported (2026-09-21).** Costs, cap, 0.25 recharge, partial-borrow spend, level-scaled exp with `(level+1)*50` upgrades to +10, cursed/`MagicImmune`/charge gates, self-target refusal, empty-cell press (trap + plant + trample), GuidingLight's 2-8 `NormalIntRange` with `Illuminated` + `WasIlluminatedTracker` on survival, the aware-crab parry (direct damage negated, add-ons still land), the boss-challenge clear, consume-on-next-landed-hit, the Cleric STR-sufficient auto-hit (plus any non-hero attacker, thrown copies included), holy `round(2 x Arcana+Berserk)` with the beneficial-enchant override (cursed affixes still proc), the mirror image's flat holy copy, ward `round(1 x Arcana)` pre-shield, the skeleton's extra -2 and the chalice-prick half (which rides the same absorb boundary). **Talent slice (2026-09-21):** the MWL Cleric T1 row is Java's own four in order; `HolyIntuition` (bag picker over unidentified curse-unknown gear, `4-points` charges, `cursedKnown` + the cursed/uncursed line, spends a turn) and `ShieldOfLight` (enemy-only seen-target gate with the `no_target` line, 4-turn tracker answering to the target's persisted id, no turn spent) join the tome window after the base three per `getSpellList`; every cast consumes a live `SatiatedSpellsTracker` (armed by eating with the talent) into a max-semantics `1+2*points` Barrier; melee hero hits on illuminated enemies detach and deal `1+2*points` Searing Light (thrown hits bypass it - `MissileWeapon` never calls `Char.attack()`); hits from the tracked enemy lose `NormalIntRange(1+points, 2+2points)` in `defenseProc` position. Strings are SPD's own v3.3.8 text including all 19 carried translations (`port.*` keys - the live checkout predates the Cleric); icons 73/74/81 sit on the byte-identical v3.3.8 buff sheets (every previously-used cell pixel-verified first); the tome art is v3.3.8's `ARTIFACT_TOME` pixels patched into transparent, unreferenced items.png cell 26 (246 there is `ARTIFACT_SPELLBOOK`, the wrong book; the sheet is otherwise the old checkout's - 215 cells differ, so no whole-sheet swap). **Simplified:** `MAGIC_BOLT`'s/`STOP_TARGET`'s unbounded ranges are the aimed-utility 8; unaffordable rows stay tappable and refuse instead of disabling; no quick-cast spell. **Closed 2026-09-22: `LIGHT_MISSILE` presentation is now real** - `MagicMissile.boltFromChar(..., LIGHT_MISSILE, hero.sprite, collisionPos, callback)` (`GuidingLight.onTargetSelected()`) needed a real moving-projectile primitive, unlike every other Cleric spell effect (all instant beams or static particle bursts): `spawnProjectile`'s existing `Projectile`/`projectiles` machinery (thrown-weapon flight) gained an optional `onArrive` callback and a new `spawnBoltTo(from, toCell, tint, onArrive)` sibling targeting an arbitrary cell rather than requiring an occupant, wired from `resolveGuidingLight` for a plain white dot with `ch.sprite.burst(0xFFFFFF44, 3)` firing on arrival (`spawnHitFlash`, Sunray's own primitive). The damage/buff resolution stays synchronous rather than deferred into the callback (Sunray's own precedent) - only the bolt and its landing burst are async. Live-verified: the bolt occupies `projectiles` while in flight and `onArrive` fires exactly once the flight completes. **Not ported:** tier 4, the PRIEST/PALADIN halves (free-cast cooldown, extends, 6/3 values, glyph/enchant keep-both), `ench_name`/`glyph_name` imbued-gear labels, the Sunray/artifactProc talent hooks, and the metamorphosed halves (the `SearingLightCooldown` zap arming, the shield's target-side -1, the non-Cleric delayed Satiated Barrier). **Corrected 2026-09-22: the "HolyWard beneficial-glyph override... not ported" clause above was wrong - it is live.** `Armor.hasGlyph()` (`items/armor/Armor.java`, tag `v3.3.8`) suppresses a beneficial glyph while `HolyWard.HolyArmBuff` is up and the hero isn't Paladin; this port's `combatResolution.ts` gates the same three places the wording already (correctly) described - the melee glyph-proc read, direct armor-glyph damage/passive reads (all keyed off `this.hero.buffs['holyWard']`), and a `syncHeroFromStats()` refresh on both cast (`castHolyBuff`) and expiry (`turnLoopAiming.ts`'s per-turn `hadHolyWard` comparison). The sentence had simply drifted into the wrong half of the row - describing finished work as a gap. **Also found and fixed in the same pass: `TOME_SPELL_COST.holyWard` was 2, matching `HolyWeapon.chargeUse()` with no Java basis** - `HolyWard.java` has no `chargeUse()` override at all, so `ClericSpell`'s own default of 1 applies (confirmed by reading both files at tag `v3.3.8`; originally flagged read-only by ACP audit-01 finding K, #479, left to the owner - fixed here). Live-verified: casting HolyWard now spends exactly 1 tome charge, not 2. **Tier-2 slice (2026-09-21):** the MWL Cleric T2 row is Java's own five in order (`ENLIGHTENING_MEAL`/`RECALL_INSCRIPTION`/`SUNRAY`/`DIVINE_SENSE`/`BLESS`); eating with the meal talent banks `(1+points)/3` of a charge on the carried tome with no exp (`directCharge` carry/cap); every used runestone reports its class from the activation path (Java's `onThrow` position - wasted fear/sleep aims and first-click intuition guesses arm too) and every successful scroll read reports its class (`readAnimation`'s chance-1 position, upgrade refused before it, transmutation on the successful reroll), arming the `UsedItemTracker` (10 turns at rank 1, 300 at rank 2, class + buff persisted); all four spells join the tome window in `getSpellList` tier-2 order with Recall's row priced off the live tracker. Recall re-casts the tracked item for free - a `talentChance = 0` scroll re-read (the effect runs, nothing consumed, no procs, no re-arm) or a fresh stone activation through a consume-dropping, re-arm-no-op context (`recastStone`; the picked transmute target stays consumed) - then detaches the tracker, no turn spent. Sunray refuses the hero's own cell, deals flat 8/12 to undead/demonic foes and `heroDamageIntRange(4, 8)`/`(6, 12)` otherwise (crab parry with the blind ladder still landing, boss-challenge clear), then the once-ever ladder (blind plus the recent marker, or paralysis detaching the marker), spending a turn. DivineSense grants the 50-turn tracker (the visibility pass already reveals through it; the explicit refresh is Java's `observe`), no turn spent. Bless takes any seen creature (self: `2+4*points` Bless plus max-semantics `5+5*points` Barrier; others: `5+5*points` Bless plus a full heal whose past-max leftover is dropped - no per-mob Barrier pool), spending a turn. The tracker icon is `GLYPH_RECALL` 78 on the byte-identical v3.3.8 buff sheets (cells 76-78 pixel-verified); its info window names the tracked item in all 19 carried locales (SPD's own translations); the three tracker countdowns ride the standard flavour overlay (the recall fade reads the rank's own 10/300). **Simplified:** aimed cells are the collision (no Ballistica, same as every other aimed port spell); exotic tracked classes refuse (this port generates no exotic scrolls, so they can never arm either); the PowerOfMany/LifeLink ally shares and the Priest illuminate need systems that do not exist here yet. Pinned headlessly (`verifyClericSpells` T1+T2 formulas, the T2 picker rows/gates/routing, `directCharge`/picker-cost/class-map units, paid-vs-free scroll/transmute arming, stone arming incl. wasted aims and first-click guesses, the buff-duration table) and live (`tools/scratch/cleric-t1-livecheck.mjs`, 17/17, zero page errors: tracker arm/consume into an exact-3 Barrier, 5-row picker order, curse reveal, 4-turn tracked shield, searing +3 and shield -3 mean distributions, all three buff icons on the v3.3.8 sheet). The `GuidingLight` aim damage/press path is now also live-exercised via its travelling-bolt presentation (2026-09-22, see above), not just headlessly. Tier 2 is pinned live by `tools/scratch/cleric-t2-livecheck.mjs` (30/30, zero page errors: tracker info window, transmute arm plus its free re-cast, meal charge, stone/scroll arming classes, 7-row picker order, free recall with tracker detach, self-refusal, the blind-then-paralyze ladder, the flat-8 undead triple, the 50-turn sense, self/other bless) with buff-sheet cells 76-78 pixel-verified. **Tier-3 slice (2026-09-21):** the MWL Cleric T3 row is Java's own pair in order (`CLEANSE`/`LIGHT_READING`, maxRank 3); `Cleanse` joins the tome window after Bless (ten rows with both earlier tiers) at the flat 2, resolving at once with no aim. The cast takes the hero plus every visible ally, sheds every negative on each (`AllyBuff`/`LostInventory` have no port model, so their exclusions are vacuous), prolongs `2*(rank-1)` immunity above rank 1 (0/2/4 - "1 less than displayed as spell is instant"), sets the hero's `10*points` Barrier (Java's `setShield` keeps the higher, the port pool's own semantics), and fires the pink flare over everyone affected; then the turn, the invisibility dispel, the satiated consume and the tome exp. The tier-3 tab opens at the level threshold on a class-authored row (`hasClassTier3Row`) - **Divergence (deliberate):** Java's `talentPointsAvailable(3)` returns 0 while `subClass == NONE` (`Hero.java`), while this port banks tier-3 points from level 13 with no subclass gate, so the literal rule would strand them until (and unless) the choice is made. `LIGHT_READING`'s live halves are the metamorphosed ones: the non-Cleric artifact shed (10/20/30% with the flare only when something detaches) and the wand-recharge leg (`1+0.2*rank/3`); the tome-tick half has no expression (no artifact equip slot - the carried tome ticks the equipped rate by the carried-cloak convention). The immunity itself (`PotionOfCleansing.Cleanse`: `IMMUNITY` icon 25 pink-tinted, the standard flavour countdown with the `DURATION`-based fade, SPD's own info text) is enforced at the shared `buffBlocked` boundary, now exported: every negative direct-write in the tree (poison-dart trap, regrowth/pharmacophobia poison, frost-bomb upgrade, wand roots, rockfall/BrightFist, dazzling/chilling/frost-imbue, lullaby/drowsy, swarm-split copies, tomahawk bleed, `setBleeding`) routes through it, matching Java's `Char.add()` refusal on every `affect` path. Strings are SPD's own v3.3.8 text in all 19 carried locales (8 keys: spell name/short_desc, both talent title/descs, buff name/desc - zero fallbacks). Pinned headlessly (`CLEANSE_COST`/immunity/shield formulas, the cleanse picker row/routing/refusal/cost/key, the `buffBlocked` cleansing matrix including `setBleeding`, the T3 unlock predicate, the cleanse halves' shed/quicken formulas, the immunity text/fade/tint pins, the hero-turn tick order) and live (`tools/scratch/cleric-t3-livecheck.mjs`, 21/21, zero page errors: 10-row order, named cleanse row, hero+ally detach, immunity 2-ticked-to-1 on the hero and full 2 on the ally, 19-shield after `Barrier.act()` decay, flares, 2-charge spend, turn spent, staged dart trap refused while still wounding, rank-1 shape, both refusals). **Simplified:** the ally `10*points` Barrier share is dropped (no per-mob pool, the Bless precedent); talent rows name the spell alone, the rank-aware cost living in the row note (`itemDisplayName` has no rank or tracker); the pink star flare is pink sparkles on the same 2s beat (no star-sprite flare here); the PowerOfMany/LifeLink ally needs systems not built here. **Not ported:** ally buff ticking (ally buffs never tick on ally turns - no buff-actor system - so immunity reads full on allies while the hero's ticks). **Closed 2026-09-22, Sunray's own cast presentation:** `new Beam.SunRay(hero.sprite.center(), collisionPos)` (Java's `1f`-duration yellow `Beam`) and `ch.sprite.burst(0xFFFFFF44, 5)` on the resolved target now fire from `resolveSunray`, reusing the same `zapBeams`/`zapBeamOverlay` primitive `WardSprite.zap()`'s `DeathRay` and every wand trail already draw (widened with a per-beam `duration` field for `SunRay`'s own `1f` against their shared `0.5f`) plus a new `spawnHitFlash` in `ui/effectBursts.ts` (the same quick-poof shape `spawnCleanseFlare` already established, tinted white). **Closed 2026-09-22, Bless's own cast flare:** `new Flare(6, 32).color(0xFFFF00, true).show(ch.sprite, 2f)` (`BlessSpell.castSpell()`) is the identical star-flare shape `Cleanse` already draws - `spawnCleanseFlare` generalized into `spawnFlare(layer, alive, x, y, color)`, fired from `resolveBless` on the resolved target (self or other) via a new `burstBlessFlare`. **Closed 2026-09-22, Judgement's screen flash and GuidingLight's travelling bolt** (see their own dedicated rows/PORT_COVERAGE entries): every Cleric tome spell's own cast presentation is now either ported or confirmed to need nothing - DivineSense/HolyWeapon/HolyWard/Flash needed none (checked directly against their Java sources). |
| PRIEST/PALADIN subclass tier-3 Cleric spells (`Radiance`/`HolyLance`/`MnemonicPrayer` Priest, `Smite`/`LayOnHands`/`AuraOfProtection`/`WallOfLight` Paladin, `HallowedGround` Priest, tag `v3.3.8`) | `src/simulation/clericSpells.ts` (cost/damage/duration formulas), `src/scenes/dungeon/hero/clericSpellFlows.ts` (`resolveRadiance`/`resolveHolyLance`/`resolvePrayer`/`resolveSmite`/`resolveLayOnHands`/`resolveAura`/`resolveHallowedGround`/`lightWallActive`/`resolveWallOfLight`), the `mnemonicExtended` save field | **Ported (2026-09-21).** Radiance illuminates every visible non-ally (bonus `lvl+5` on one already illuminated, then Paralysis 3 on survivors) and grants the hero's own Light for 100 turns (20 under DARKNESS). HolyLance and Smite land direct spell damage (undead/demonic at the roll's max) on the confirmed cell, no Ballistica, the same convention as Sunray; HolyLance arms a 30-turn `lanceCooldown`. MnemonicPrayer extends an ally's (or the hero's) present `MNEMONIC_POSITIVE_BUFFS` by `2+points`, or an enemy's Illuminated + negative buffs the same amount, for free; the hero's own once-per-buff-instance gate (`mnemonicExtended`, cleared when that buff itself detaches) is ported, saved and loaded. LayOnHands heals/shields an adjacent target for free; AuraOfProtection grants the 20-turn buff. **Correction 2026-09-21:** the aura damage-reduction clause IS ported - see the `AuraOfProtection.AuraBuff` row below (`auraProtectedDamage` at the attack gate and the bomb/blob/trap seams); the stale "no hook" claim predates that row. **Divergence (deliberate):** `HallowedGround`'s standing, grass-evolving `HallowedTerrain` patch and `WallOfLight`'s standing blocking cross-wall have no port model (no terrain-panel system to place/tick either); both land as one-shot instant effects instead - HallowedGround heals/shields allies and roots enemies once over its square the instant it is cast, WallOfLight stuns whoever is standing across the aimed line's perpendicular span instead of placing a lasting wall, tracked by the port-only `lightWallActive` buff so the free early-end recast (`WallOfLight.chargeUse()`) still works. Smite's real weapon-swing integration and `+300%` enchant power (`SMITE_ENCHANT_BONUS`) are likewise not ported - the bonus lands as the whole hit. Pinned in `test:simulation`/`test:items` (cost/gate/duration formulas, the mock `HolyTomeContext`); not yet browser-verified live. |

The Cleric has no counterpart in this checkout, so the port unlocks it on first victory. |

Live proc families now include barrier/shield absorption, healing triggers, food bonuses, stealth shielding, combo/execute, point-blank ranged **accuracy** (see below), charge refunds, lethal-momentum/free-turn effects, follow-up strikes, heightened senses, durable projectiles, nature's aid/bounty, rejuvenating steps, weapon recharging and Iron Will mitigation. **Corrected 2026-09-15: `POINT_BLANK` is not a damage talent.** It appears exactly once in Java, inside `MissileWeapon.adjacentAccFactor`, as `0.5f + 0.25f*pointsInTalent(POINT_BLANK)` replacing the flat `0.5f` melee-range penalty for thrown weapons and the spirit bow, and its range test is `adjacent` (Chebyshev 1), not `distance <= 2`; this row and the proc-heavy row below both used to say "point-blank ranged damage/scaling", and the code applied `1 + 0.2*rank` damage at `<= 2`. Now ported as accuracy - on the throw path since 2026-09-15, and on the bow branch since 2026-09-18, which also deleted the `1 + 0.2*rank` damage residue the earlier pass had left behind there (the premature "both ranged paths" claim is corrected accordingly) - see the `MissileWeapon` ranged-accuracy row for the formulas and the live verification. Exact Java formulas for the remaining rare ability talents remain roadmap work |

The wording is SPD's own v3.3.8 text pulled from the tag per locale into `portStrings.ts` (the port's usual route for strings this checkout's own message files predate - see the `Symbol`/i18n rows), not retyped or machine-translated. Java's extra `!cursed || (seal.getGlyph() != null && seal.getGlyph().curse())` escape needs a seal-glyph model this port lacks, so a cursed incoming armor refuses outright with the real `items.brokenseal.cursed_armor` line. **Browser-verified live** (2026-09-15, Italian catalogue): a Warrior whose seal was affixed equipping a fresh armor opened the window with the seal's own name and SPD's text (`sì`/`no`); clicking `sì` through the real pointer path re-sealed the new armor (`armorSealed` false -> true, window closed); and equipping a cursed armor instead logged `Il sigillo non si attaccherà ad un armatura maledetta.` with no window and no seal. **`AC_DETACH` is ported too (2026-09-15).** Tapping the equipped armor in the inventory (where Java lists the action) returns the seal to the bag, logs SPD's real `items.armor.armor.detach_seal` line - verified live in the game's own language: "Distacchi il sigillo dalla tua armatura." - clears the sealed state and re-syncs the Warrior's shield; a second tap with no seal attached does nothing, and Java's drop-at-the-hero's-feet fallback (a full bag) has no case here. Still unported: the seal's own curse glyph from the Blacksmith's reforge. |

Scroll upgrades now preserve that tier and add Java's plain +1 level; because this port has no item-picker modal, the action auto-targets the lower-level equipped weapon or armor, and ammo users let missiles catch up after both equipped items reach that level. **Corrected 2026-09-15: the three "remain open"/"remain gaps" clauses this row ended on were all stale** - Blacksmith transfer (`transferEnhancement`) has been ported and used by both equip paths, hardening (`enchantHardened`/`glyphHardened` + the Blacksmith's harden service) is ported and was re-verified line-for-line against tag `v3.3.8`, equipped-slot targeting is in both infusion pickers (see the `MagicalInfusion` row), and the Curse Infusion bonus is now Java's virtual `1 + level/6` with the real clearing rule (see the dedicated row).
