| Spare carried wands with per-wand charges | `items/wands.ts`, `simulation/spareWands.ts`, `test:items`, `test:simulation` | Ported (2026-09-21, foundation half of the Mage's two remaining armor abilities): every carried wand now owns Java's `maxCharges`/`curCharges`/`partialCharge` (`Wand.java`, tag `v3.3.8` - `min(initialCharges + level, 10)`, Magic Missile 3, the rest 2, arriving full). A ground pickup of the wielded class (or unknown class) absorbs exactly as before; any *other* class lands as a spare entry with its own identity and full charge state instead of being silently destroyed. Spares recharge each hero turn on the shared pool's own rate formula (per-wand `Charger`s share it in Java) and persist through the `bagSources` side channel. `WildMagic`'s selection (shuffle, cost gate, seconds/thirds top-up to `4 + FIRE_EVERYTHING`) and per-shot spend (`partialCharge` first, borrowing whole charges) are ported pure and pinned. **Firing ported 2026-09-21**: `activateWildMagic` spends the 25 armor charge, fires every selected spare through the extracted `fireWandShot` (the normal zap's own body, so all thirteen classes keep their exact effects) at the Wild-Power-boosted level with `chargesPerCast` forced to 1, re-aiming per shot at whoever stands on the cell, then dispels invisibility and spends the turn unless `Int(4)` rolls under CONSERVED_MAGIC - all pinned in `test:armorAbilities`. Simplifications: the staff is excluded (Java excludes it too); self-aim refuses silently (the port's Shockwave convention - neither `self_target` nor `prompt` key exists in the catalog); empty-cell aims refuse with `no_target` (Java fires at bare cells, whose only observable is terrain FX this port's creature-bound shots cannot produce). **Imbue ported 2026-09-21**: the Mage tapping a spare chooses wield (that entry, fixing the old first-found equip) or imbue; imbue refuses unidentified (`id_first`) and cursed (`cursed`, matching Java's own refusal) spares, confirms with the real `imbue_desc` level (the genuine `imbueStaffLevel` sync through `weaponLevel`) plus `imbue_lost`/`imbue_cursed` where due, consumes the spare into the staff with the `imbue` line, and persists through save/load (default Magic Missile, Java's starting imbue). The old imbue is lost outright - Java's `WAND_PRESERVATION` eject has no system here. **Blast offered and fired 2026-09-21**: `activateElementalBlast` reads the imbue, aims the roomiest cardinal, and runs the full 360-degree cone with every class effect (cell FX, damage with armor, statuses, knockback, heals, guardian feed, self-effects, reactive barrier) through the pinned arithmetic - see the row's simplification list in the method comment. **Frost-leg correction 2026-09-21 (opencode-01, via ACP #81 caller-side note)**: the frost leg routed through `addBuff(mob, 'chill', ...)` - a single Chill that could only freeze after sustained re-application - where Java (`ElementalBlast.java`, tag `v3.3.8`) does `Buff.affect(mob, Frost.class, effectMulti*Frost.DURATION)` outright. It now applies Frost at the scaled duration directly, detaches Burning up front and Chill plus a max-kept Paralysis clock on a successful attach (Java's `Frost.attachTo` order), pinned at source level in `test:armorAbilities`. Residuals, stated: the Thief-stolen-potion/carried-meat freeze inside `attachTo` has no per-item-freeze primitive on this seam; the `chilling`-enchant leg nearby stays on `applyChillFreeze` because Java seeds a real `Freezing` blob there, which this port lacks (documented blob simplification, not this bug). Every class now offers its full armor-ability set. |
| `CursedWand.cursedZap()` (`items/wands/CursedWand.java`, tag `v3.3.8`) | `simulation/cursedWand.ts` (the pure tier/effect-pick tables), `castCursedWandEffect`/`castCursedWandCommonEffect`/`castCursedWandUncommonEffect` in `src/scenes/dungeon/hero/cursedWandCast.ts` (moved out of `armorAbilityUse.ts` once that group outgrew its own file budget, the file-size refactor's next extraction, behavior-identical), called from `activateWildMagic` | **Ported 2026-09-21, scoped and documented as such rather than left as a total exclusion** (superseding the "no cursed-wand-backfire mechanic in this port" note elsewhere in this file). Real Java rolls a weighted tier first (`EFFECT_CAT_CHANCES = {60, 30, 9, 1}` common/uncommon/rare/v.rare) then a uniform pick within it - roughly twenty `CursedEffect` classes across the four tiers in total. This port models the Common, Uncommon and Rare tiers at Java's own real 60/30/9 weights (`pickCursedTier`, VeryRare's remaining 1% folded into Rare - see the module doc comment for why that's a no-observable-difference approximation), and a subset of each tier's real effect list, every one reusing existing port infrastructure with real Java values. **Common, 6 of 8**: `BurnAndFreeze` (coin-flip Burning/Frost split between user and target), `RandomTeleport` (a live non-`IMMOVABLE` target teleports on a second coin flip, else the caster does, reusing the `randomFreeCell`/`moveTo`/`playTeleportAppear` triple the Displacing curse already uses), `RandomGas` (`Random.Int(3)` between ConfusionGas 800 / ToxicGas 500 / ParalyticGas 200, Java's exact volumes, seeded at the bolt's own collision cell via the existing named blob fields), `RandomWand` (a uniformly-picked class from this port's full 13-wand `WAND_TYPES` roster - matching Java's real `Generator.Category.WAND` list one for one - fired once through the existing `fireWandShot`), `SelfOoze` (every character within a 2-cell walkable-distance flood of the caster, via `pathfinder.distanceMap`, gets Ooze at its full duration), and `Bubbles` as a genuine no-op beyond the tier draw (Java's own effect is a harmless particle burst plus an arbitrary-cell press this port has no seam for - "fun, harmless" is the intended shape, not a gap). **Uncommon, all 8, added across four follow-up passes the same day**: `HealthTransfer` (`scalingDepth()*2` raw damage on a coin-flip loser, half healed to the winner - `this.depth` standing in for `scalingDepth()` as usual, routed through `absorbHeroDamage`/`kill` on the hero side and a plain hp write plus `kill` on the monster side; no badge system exists here for Java's friendly/enemy-magic death distinction on this specific kill, so that half is silently dropped rather than faked), `Geyser` (reuses the exact `activateGeyserTrapFlow` the port's own 'geyser' utility trap already calls), `SummonSheep` (reuses the exact distance-2-flood-of-free-cells `spawnSheep` loop the port's own 'flock' utility trap already runs, live-verified spawning 14 sheep across 14 free cells in an open room, and correctly spawning none in a boxed-in one - the real Java "no space, no sheep" outcome, not a bug), `Levitate` (a live, not-already-flying, non-`IMMOVABLE` target gets Levitation; otherwise the caster does - "already flying" reads the same live `buffs['levitation']`/`FLYING_KINDS` check every other flight site in this port uses, since there is no per-instance `.flying` write site to touch), `Alarm` (every hostile mob wakes and gets `lastSeen` set to the caster's cell, reusing the port's own 'alarm' utility trap's exact wake shape), and `RandomPlant` (a uniformly-picked plant kind from the real 12-kind supported roster - `Generator.randomUsingDefaults(SEED)` has no equivalent weighted-draw seam here, so a uniform pick stands in, the same precedent `RandomWand`'s `WAND_TYPES` pick set - seeded at the bolt's own collision cell via the exact `manualPlants.set`/`placePortedFeature` pair the hero's own `plantSeed` action uses, generalized off the hero's own cell; refuses silently on an occupied feature/chasm/impassable cell, matching Java's own `valid()` gate. Live-verified spawning all 12 kinds across a 93-free-cell sweep with zero errors, and correctly refusing a cell that already held a just-planted one), and `Explosion` (`new Bomb.ConjuredBomb().explode(pos)` - Java's `ConjuredBomb` is an empty `Bomb` subclass with zero overrides, so it resolves exactly this port's 'standard' `MWL_BOMB_RULES` entry: terrain-burn, ground-item-chain, then per-character damage, all three reusing `detonateBomb`'s own three base-blast loops rather than being re-derived - the newly-exported `applyBlastDamage` (previously module-private) already covers the hero branch and every boss-hook/King-shield/PhantomPiranha/Yog/Tengu edge case `detonateBomb` itself relies on, so this needed no new logic, only an export and a loop at the bolt's own cell in place of a ground item's. Live-verified: a scripted sweep showed the hero and an adjacent rat taking simultaneous, independently-rolled damage (`Random.normalRange(4 + depth, 12 + 3*depth)` each) with zero errors - the earlier claim that this needed "a bomb-blast helper that also hits the hero" was wrong; `applyBlastDamage` always had one, it just wasn't exported yet), and `LightningBolt` (turned out mostly presentation once read past the sprite calls: every `Lightning()` visual and `ScrollOfRecharging.charge()` are pure particle bursts with zero mechanical effect in `v3.3.8` - the "genuinely large multi-area effect" this row used to call it was an overestimate made without reading that far. The real mechanical shape is the union of NEIGHBOURS9 around the caster's cell and the bolt's collision cell, deduplicated by identity; the hero in that union gets an *additional* Recharging grant (full port duration standing in for Java's `Recharging.DURATION/3`) on top of - not instead of - the same unconditional armor-piercing `NormalIntRange(5 + depth/4, 10 + depth/4)` `Electricity` damage plus Paralysis every other affected character takes, since `positiveOnly` [the only thing that would exempt an ally] is never true here. Reuses `applyBlastDamage` a third time with `pierceArmor: true`. Live-verified: a scripted sweep showed the hero and an adjacent rat taking simultaneous damage with Recharging/Paralysis landing on both in the same tick, zero errors), and `MassInvuln` (Rare, 1 of 8 - every character on the level gets Invulnerability 10 and a full Bless, both already-modeled buffs needing no new infrastructure at all; live-verified on a fresh page giving both a spawned rat and the hero exactly `invulnerability: 10, bless: 30`), and `ConeOfColors` (Rare, 2 of 8, added 2026-09-21: Java re-does the bolt `STOP_SOLID` - so it passes through characters - then builds an 8-radius, 90-degree `ConeAOE` from it; this port's `coneCells` (`mechanics/cone.ts`) with `coneRay`'s `stopAtTarget: false` is that same stop mode, already used by Shockwave/Regrowth. Every affected character except the caster (excluded exactly like Java's `if (cell == user.pos) continue;`) gets an independently-rolled `NormalIntRange(5+depth, 10+depth*2)` hit and a uniformly-picked one of Java's five statuses - Burning (`reigniteBuff`), Frost (buff only if the hit is survived, matching Java's `if (ch.isAlive())` order), Poison (`Poison.set`'s real max-with-current write, `3+depth/2`), Ooze (buff before damage, matching Java's order), or Electricity+Paralysis (buff only if survived) - all five reusing existing status primitives with no new infrastructure. Damage is a direct HP write (hero via `absorbHeroDamage`, others via a plain subtract) rather than `applyBlastDamage`, matching `HealthTransfer`'s precedent since Java's own `ch.damage(dmg, source)` here passes the buff instance as source, not `CursedWand.class`, so this port's `magicImmune` (its AntiMagic-glyph stand-in) correctly does not gate it either - confirmed by reading `AntiMagic.RESISTS`, which lists `CursedWand.class` itself but not `Burning`/`Frost`/`Poison`/`Ooze`/`Electricity`. `positiveOnly`'s ally exemption and `tryForWandProc`'s generic wand-glyph reaction hook are never reachable from WildMagic and are not modeled, matching every other tier's stated convention. Pins in `test:simulation` (`pickConeOfColorsStatus`/`CURSED_RARE_EFFECT_IDS`) and `test:armorAbilities` (source-level cone/dispatch checks). **Browser-verified live 2026-09-21** (an isolated scratch build, since the shared tree was red on a concurrent session's unrelated in-flight catalyst-retirement slice at the time - throwaway stub exports satisfied the bundler around it without touching that session's claimed files): ~220 scripted `castCursedWandRareEffect` calls landed the tier split near its real 50/50 (of the two ported ids at the time) weight, exercised all five ConeOfColors statuses with correct independent damage rolls and kills, correctly excluded the hero from its own cone, zero console errors), and `SheepPolymorph` (Rare, 3 of 8, added 2026-09-21: a live, non-hero, non-boss/miniboss, non-NPC target at the bolt's collision cell - Java's exact `valid()` gate, read off this port's `BOSS_KINDS`/`MINIBOSS_KINDS`/`isNPC` - is silently destroyed with no death and no loot, reusing `destroyAlly`'s own no-death teardown shape (`scheduler.remove`, creature-array splice, sprite destroy/unregister), then replaced with a fresh 10-turn `spawnSheep` at its cell, reusing `SummonSheep`'s own factory. An ineligible or missing target is a genuine no-op, matching Java's own gate rather than a substitute target. Pins in `test:simulation`/`test:armorAbilities`. **Browser-verified live 2026-09-21** (same isolated scratch-build technique as `ConeOfColors` above - a first attempt was invalidated by a stale duplicate local server still bound to the same port and serving an old build, caught by fetching `/game.js` and diffing its content against the freshly-built file rather than trusting the browser's rendered state alone): 200 scripted `castCursedWandRareEffect` calls against a spawned rat produced sheep with Java's exact `sheepTurns: 10`/`hp: maxHp: 1` and a registered sprite, while 30 calls against a boss-kind target (`goo`) never produced a sheep once, confirming the `BOSS_KINDS` gate; zero console errors throughout). **Not ported**: `SpawnRegrowth` and `RandomAreaEffect`'s `ChillingTrap` sub-case (Common) both need a generic pluggable blob class (`Regrowth`/`Freezing`) - every blob in this port is a named scene field with its own bespoke evolve hook, not a pluggable type, so adding either is real new infrastructure, not an effect-table entry; Rare's other five (`SummonMonsters`/`CurseEquipment` need `SummoningTrap`/`CursingTrap` kinds declared in `TrapKind`'s union but never actually implemented - a separate, pre-existing gap; `Petrify` needs a `TimeStasis` buff that doesn't exist; `InterFloorTeleport` needs real weighted-depth floor-travel wiring; `FireBall` needs an arbitrary-point FOV cast plus a knockback primitive); the whole VeryRare tier (folded into Rare's odds, not separately modeled); `WondrousResin`'s `positiveOnly` mode (no such artifact exists here); `tryForWandProc`'s Arcane Vision/SoulMark hook (belongs to unported systems, same gap already stated for the ordinary zap path); `RandomWand` inherits `fireWandShot`'s own `magicImmune` guard automatically (same function as the ordinary zap path), and `HealthTransfer`'s own direct damage write is now gated the same way (**fixed same day**: `CursedWand` is one of `AntiMagic.RESISTS`' listed source classes, so its damage half must zero against a `magicImmune` victim while the heal half still lands unconditionally - the earlier `RESISTS` row's "no code exists to add a guard to" note for `CursedWand` is now stale, closing that exception). Wired into `activateWildMagic`'s firing loop: a cursed spare now calls `castCursedWandEffect` in place of `fireWandShot`, and runs even with no live creature at the collision cell (several Common effects don't need one, unlike the ordinary zap branch) - browser-verified live six times (built `dist/`, `chrome-devtools-mcp`): the Common pass (300 scripted calls) exercised all six branches with zero errors and showed real buff icons on the live status bar; the Uncommon pass (500 scripted calls, two rooms) exercised the first five branches with zero errors, including a real hero death through the `HealthTransfer` damage path and the sheep-flood space-dependent outcome above; the `RandomPlant`, `Explosion`, `LightningBolt` and `MassInvuln` follow-ups are covered in their own entries above (the `MassInvuln` check also caught and correctly diagnosed a stress-test-loop contamination artifact from a long-lived browser page as not a code bug, confirmed by a fresh reload). `tsc`/`npm run verify`/`build` green; pins in `test:simulation` (the pure tables) and `test:armorAbilities` (the wiring, source-level since the scene is Pixi-bound). |
| Tengu fire cone burning terrain | `seedTenguConeCell` (`scenes/dungeon/bosses/bossLogic.ts`) | **Divergence (deliberate):** Java's `Tengu.FireAbility.FireBlob.evolve()` decrements and ignites creatures but never calls `Level.destroy()` and never spreads, so the cone leaves grass and doors standing where ordinary fire would reduce them to embers. This port seeds ordinary fire instead, so the cone burns terrain exactly like the rest - see `tools/scratch/FLAMABLE-INVENTORY.md` item 1. Residual: the seeded fire also burns Tengu himself, where Java's blob never does. |
| Burning/ooze extinguish timing in water | hero DoT tick (`scenes/dungeon/turnLoopAiming.ts`) | **Divergence (deliberate), timing only:** Java's `Burning.act()` extinguishes on the `acted && water && !flying` cliff - one turn late, so a bearer that never acts (stunned, paused) keeps burning in water. This port extinguishes immediately once the turn's tick has landed, and the flying gate (`levitation`) and the ooze wash stand as Java has them. See `tools/scratch/FLAMABLE-INVENTORY.md` item 4. |
| Blob-cell presentation (fire/gas cell particles) | `syncBlobCells` in `ui/effectBursts.ts`, bound from `updateEffectBursts` | **Simplified (2026-09-21):** active fire, toxic/corrosive gas, freezing, web and smoke cells now emit tinted, FOV-gated particles with Java-like continuous cadence. The port uses one generic white emitter rather than Java's `FireParticle`/`SnowParticle`/gas `Speck` factories, and the remaining blob kinds still have no dedicated cell emitter; blob tick ordering follows this port's turn loop rather than Java's `Blob`-array act order. |
| `Piranha.act()`/`PhantomPiranha.damage()`/`Piranha.random()` (`actors/mobs/Piranha.java`, `PhantomPiranha.java`, tag `v3.3.8`) | `phantomPiranha` MWL kind, aquarium/pool room roll, piranha movement/death gates, `combatResolution.ts` damage/teleport, `simulation/pourAuras.ts`, `test:simulation` | **Ported (audited 2026-09-21):** both fish variants use Java's depth-scaled stats, speed 2, water-only patrol/pursuit and land death; aquarium/pool rooms make Java's one-in-50 `Piranha.random()` draw and instantiate the distinct PhantomPiranha, whose incoming remote and direct blast/blob damage is halved and which teleports after surviving damage to free water (preferring cells adjacent to a character attacker, then unseen water), drops PhantomMeat, and pours the Java light sparkle cadence. **Simplified:** the PhantomPiranha uses the shared piranha texture with a static variant frame (its dedicated 21+ animation clips and attack ripple are not represented); source-less direct seams use Java's random-water branch because bomb/blob/trap callers do not retain a `Char` source, while source-aware Wand/Cleric damage is not yet separated from those seams. |
### Current correction: EnhanceBomb

The ten `Bomb.EnhanceBomb` ingredient/result pairs are now executable through the authored
alchemy picker. The base blast follows Java's own `explosionRange()` per subclass (1 for a plain
bomb, 2 for Frost/Fire/Flashbang/Shock/Woolly/Holy/Noisemaker, 3 for Regrowth, 8 for Shrapnel),
and Regrowth, Arcane and Shrapnel no longer receive it at all - all three override
`explodesDestructively()` to false in Java, so giving them the base blast was an undeclared
divergence. Arcane then rolls its own armor-piercing `NormalIntRange(4+scalingDepth,
12+3*scalingDepth)`, Shrapnel the same roll minus the target's armor out to Chebyshev 8 (Java shadow-casts the FOV; the port has no blast-centered FOV service - recorded open 2026-09-19),
and Regrowth heals (through the ordinary `PotionOfHealing.cure()`/`heal()` pair, hero only - the
port has no standing ally side) instead of damaging anything; healing every monster in the area
was a plain bug. Holy's bonus is Java's own `Math.round(NormalIntRange(scalingDepth+4,
12+3*scalingDepth) * 0.5f)` (the previous 5+depth..10+2*depth range was invented), and Frost,
Fire, Flashbang, Shock and Woolly still reuse the existing chill/fire/status/sheep seams. Every
one of these effects deliberately uses a Chebyshev circle where Java builds a PathFinder distance
map or a ShadowCaster field of view, shared statuses rather than Java's exact blob/bolt/blindness
subsystems, and three sheep rather than the real spawn field and lifetimes. Noisemaker's own fuse
is now ported (`tickBombFuses`): its 2-turn fuse arms the alarm instead of exploding, an armed
unit detonates as soon as any character stands on its cell, it re-screams every 6 acts, cannot be
picked up or snuffed once armed, and keeps acting through a Timekeeper freeze
(`NoisemakerFuse.freeze()`). Its scream reuses the port's `Mob.beckon(pos)` stand-in, so it wakes
and turns the level's mobs instead of sending them to the bomb's cell, and the Java alert
sound/scream particle are not reproduced; the state (`noisemakerArmed`/`noisemakerAlertIn`) rides
the heap payload and so survives save/load. Picking up any lit bomb now snuffs it, not only a
plain `bomb` (a lit specialty bomb used to keep its `fuseTurns` in the bag). The crystal
pool is now enforced, but its scrap/add UI and blast particles/sound remain open. GooBlob and
MetalShard identities are authored, their Java value/energy metadata is represented, and
Goo/DM-300 now drop 2/3/4 materials with the real 60/30/10 distribution. The port's
one-item-per-cell placement is a documented heap simplification.

**2026-09-19 correction: ShockBomb out, SmokeBomb in.** The ten pairs above matched the live
checkout's Java tree, which predates `v2.5.3` ("buffed all alchemy bombs" deleted
`ShockBomb.java`; `4.0.0-beta` still has no ShockBomb). At tag `v3.3.8` the map reads
invisibility -> `SmokeBomb` and recharging -> `FlashBangBomb`, both cost 2, and the recipes now
match that: `enhanceBombSmoke` (bomb + invisibility) and `enhanceBombFlashbang` (bomb +
recharging). The `smokeBomb` id replaces `shockBomb` everywhere (item, specialty category,
`value()` 60 = `quantity * (20 + 40)`); a save carrying the old id still loads (bag payloads
are id-tolerant) but the fossil no longer brews, throws, or detonates. Its blast is the shared
`super.explode()` plus `SmokeScreen` 40 per distance-2 flood cell *including* the center, with
the unplaced share of the 1000-volume budget piled onto the center on top of its own 40
(`smokeBombSeedPlan`, center total 40 plus remainder - pinned in `test:simulation`). The fog itself is a persisted blob advancing
through the shared diffusion, and its whole game effect is sight: `pruneSmokeFromSight` /
`smokeBlocksSight` mirror `Level.updateFieldOfView` (smoke strictly between viewer and target
blocks; smoky endpoints stay visible) for the hero's merged sight, ordinary-mob `seesHero`
and ally-target queries, the necromancer's skeleton placement, and the fist-teleport search -
allies keep their unpruned `allyFov` exactly as Java exempts them (no geomancer kind exists
to exempt). The ray is MWG's Bresenham `traceLine`, an approximation of Java's `ShadowCaster`
that only ever removes visibility, never adds it; like every gas here the cloud itself has no
tile art, so the closing fog is the feedback. Names live under `port.name.smokebomb` /
`port.desc.smokebomb` carrying SPD's own `v3.3.8` words in all 19 `PORT_STRINGS` locales
(byte-audited against the tag), because the generated catalogue still predates the swap and
has no `smokebomb` keys - they flip to `items.bombs.smokebomb.*` when it is re-extracted.
Stated gaps, not silent: the ChaoticCenser trinket stays open (no trinket system here). **2026-09-19 follow-up: the `ShroudingFog` exotic is ported too** - `potionShrouding` brewed from one `potionInvis` for 4 energy (`PotionToExotic`, value regular + 20 = 50, energy regular + 4 = 10, identified-state inheritance per `ExoticPotion.isKnown`), quaffing shatters Java's 180-per-cell NEIGHBOURS8 ring with the center top-up into the persisted `SmokeScreen` blob (`Potion.apply()`'s own default is `shatter(hero.pos)`; Java logs nothing on the shatter, so neither does this). Transmutation flips it back to `potionInvis` (`exoToReg`) without joining the random deck.
**2026-09-19 follow-up: the flashbang rework is ported too.** The daze is gone; every char in
the flood takes a fresh `NormalIntRange(4 + depth/2, 6 + depth)` quartered as `Electricity`
damage (armor-piercing, like Java's raw `damage()` call) plus a `Paralysis` prolong, with no
LOS gate. Two reductions ride along: the prolong lands the port's 3-turn paralysis rather
than Java's 10 (global buff-table reduction), and the Ring-of-Elements electric resistance
the blob path models does not reach the bomb adapter. Name/desc move to
`port.name.flashbang`/`port.desc.flashbang` carrying SPD's own `v3.3.8` `flashbangbomb` words
in all 19 locales (byte-audited like the smoke keys - the catalogue still ships the
pre-`v3.3.8` blinder text), and `value()` moves 15 -> 50 (`quantity * (20 + 30)`), pinned in
`test:items`.
Type-check/check/build/item/simulation suites green; browser verification owed per ROADMAP.md
section 10.
| `Challenge.SpectatorFreeze` damage immunity (`actors/hero/abilities/duelist/Challenge.java`, `Char.isInvulnerable`, tag `v3.3.8`) | `combatResolution` attack gate, `bombEffects.applyBlastDamage`, `panelsSingleUse.applyBlastDamage`, `environmentFireTraps` rockfall/explosive blast and fire/blob bindings, `simulation/geyserTrap` | Ported (2026-09-21): Java's spectator buff makes the frozen non-hero invulnerable to every `Char.damage()` source, not merely melee attacks, while its `BlobImmunity` list also blocks Fire/EternalFire and every harmful environmental blob. The port now preserves the damage/armor RNG rolls but discards HP loss at each direct bomb/blast/trap seam, including fiery Geyser victims, preserves Rockfall's separate paralysis and Geyser's douse/push tail, and includes spectator freeze in Fire/blob immunity. The spectator's darkened sprite and the full no-new-buffs immunity lock remain presentation/model gaps. |
| `Honeypot.shatter()` / `Bee.setPotInfo()` (tag `v3.3.8`) | `src/items/honeypot.ts`, `src/scenes/dungeon/panelsSingleUse.ts` | **Corrected 2026-09-21:** when an occupied target forces the hostile Bee onto a free cardinal neighbour, the port now keeps the Bee's persistent `potPos` anchored to the original shattered cell while its spawn position is the neighbour, matching Java's separate `newPos` and `setPotInfo(pos, owner)`. The existing strike-back, honeyed-charm, `ShatteredPot`, and pit-landing residuals remain stated in the Honeypot matrix. |
| `Hero.search(true/false)` radius/secret sweep (`actors/hero/Hero.java`, tag `v3.3.8`) | `src/simulation/search.ts`, `src/adapters/gameSimulation.ts`, `src/scenes/dungeon/environmentFireTraps.ts`, `src/scenes/dungeon/actorTurnsHazards.ts`, `tools/verifySearch.mjs` | **Corrected 2026-09-21:** intentional search uses Java's Rogue base radius 2 (5x5), Wide Search rank-1 circle/rank-2 square, and reveals every secret in scan order. Successful movement now invokes passive `search(false)`, with Java's depth-scaled trap/door probabilities, visibility gate, tutorial gate, cursed-Talisman suppression, and equipped-Talisman Foresight sweep. The port has no separate Foresight buff object, so an equipped, non-immune Talisman is the equivalent active state (including its cursed scan radius; curse only suppresses ordinary passive detection); Java's mapping/awareness particles and non-searchable-trap subtype remain presentation/model gaps. |
| `AuraOfProtection.AuraBuff` damage reduction (`Char.attack()`/`Char.damage()`, tag `v3.3.8`) | `auraProtectedDamage` in `src/simulation/clericSpells.ts`, `combatResolution`, bomb/blob/trap damage seams | **Ported (2026-09-21):** active Paladin aura now reduces incoming damage to the hero and nearby same-alignment allies by Java's 10/20/30% factor, after character attack multipliers and before defender curves, and on direct bomb/blob damage seams. The compact port uses hero/ally/NPC flags for Java's `ALLY` alignment and Chebyshev distance 2 for the path-distance check; both are documented geometry/model reductions. **Follow-up 2026-09-21:** Java's defend-side twin `Armor.Glyph.genericProcChanceMultiplier()` (`Armor.java` 821-831) adds `+0.25 + 0.25*rank` for the same gate, which the shared `genericProcMultiplier()` (Java's `Weapon`-side shape: Arcana + Berserk/catalyst) never carried - every defend-side glyph seam (stone, displacement, repulsion, antimagic, viscosity, the HolyWard block, obfuscation stealth) now routes through `armorProcMultiplier`, while attacker-side rolls (enchants, kinetic, holy-weapon bonus, projecting reach, mob on-hit hooks) stay on the old shape. The LifeLink-buff proximity alternative is vacuous (no such buff exists here). Pinned in `test:simulation` (`verifyClericSpells`). |
| `Skeleton.die()` bone explosion (`actors/mobs/Skeleton.java`, tag `v3.3.8`) | `skeletonBoneExplosion` in `deathSaveRefresh.ts`, `simulation/skeletonExplosion.ts` | **Ported (2026-09-21):** ordinary and NecroSkeleton deaths now roll Java's 6..12 damage for each living character in the eight neighbouring cells, subtract two defender armor rolls, and route the result through the shared defender/HP/death seams; falling deaths skip the blast. The port retains the generated `actors.mobs.skeleton.explo_kill` message when the hero dies. **Simplified:** Java's second application of RockArmor/Earthroot reduction and its target-health-indicator talent branch have no equivalent state in this compact port. |
